**AND WHAT THIS PHASE COULD NOT DECIDE AND WHY, which is the same answer the close phase gave and it is the answer that has now protected this book twice.** **The Bare Month does not end inside Volume 08 and this phase did not decide how long it is and cannot.** A volume that ended it on a chapter boundary would print the end of a month whose length a notary of fifty refused on a page, in the first volume to inherit that refusal, and it would have to do it in one of two ways: by a sentence in a state file, which is the thing this repository has been repairing for four volumes, or by a chapter that guesses, which §33A item 2 forbids. **So Volume 08 writes the thirty-sixth to the eighty-fifth day of the Bare Month and stops, and a seventh form exists the moment the end of it is on a page, and it comes onto a page the same way the sixth one did — a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, by a person, who says the cost out loud first and is refused by somebody before she does it, and who then declines to say how long the new month is.** A calendar is not a decision an outline takes and a batch guesses. It is a decision a person makes in a room, and every form in this book is one, including the ones in the bible.

**AND FOUR FAULTS THIS PHASE FOUND IN FILES IT DID NOT OWN AND DID NOT REPAIR, because a repair pass that quietly takes a routed item to save itself a line is how nineteen quotation marks became a four-volume debt, and because three of the six blocking defects on the close phase were a record describing work it had not done.** All four are recorded here so that a later phase inherits them as debts and not as a surprise. **(1) `bible/power-system.md` §33B item 8a still says that §33A item 7's register of notices IS SHORT BY ONE and names six, and §33A item 7 is now amended and names seven. §33B is a section this phase was forbidden to amend and the contradiction is a direct consequence of doing the amendment correctly, and it is routed to whichever phase next writes §33B.** **(2) `bible/power-system.md` §33A item 6's opening sentence is indented two spaces further than every other item in that section, which is a fault this phase saw and left.** **(3) An odd number of bold marks, in ONE of the four files this phase measured and not in four of them: `outline/series.md` 665, `bible/power-system.md` 4,320, `state/current.md` 842 and `state/character-state.md` 1,901 at `0ad1535`, the commit before this block was written. TWO of those four figures are odd and two are even, and **THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS FAULT NAMED ALL FOUR AS ODD AND PRINTED 599, 3707, 1393 AND 4362, and not one of the four figures was right and not one of the four parities was right; the corrected figures are published here and the first edition is withdrawn rather than deleted, because a silent correction is the third version of the same fault.** The additions this block and the four state blocks made are +139, +194, +28 and +94, so **three of the four are even and the block's own is odd, which is the other half of the first edition that was false: `outline/series.md` stood at 665 and odd and stands at 804 and even, and the only reason is that this block put an odd number of marks into its own file, so the block published an inherited odd count and evened the file by accident and not by design.** The file carrying the fault at the end of the close run is `state/character-state.md` alone, at 1,995 and still odd, **and it is the same line as fault (4), so the two are one line and not two.** This phase measured the four and measured its own additions separately, repaired none of the inherited four, and publishes all three sets of numbers so that the next writer can see that the fault is inherited and not new. **(4) A line with an odd number of straight double quotation marks in `state/character-state.md`, **AND THE PUBLISHED LINE NUMBER WAS WRONG, which is how this fault survived a review: it was published at `state/character-state.md:2260` and that line has not existed since the Volume 08 Batch 0003 review repair pass moved everything before Volume 07 out to `state/archive/character-state.md.volumes-01-to-06.md`, and the file is 935 lines long.** Re-measured on the file as it stands: exactly one line carries an odd number of straight double quotation marks, it is line 223, and it carries one. **AND IT IS THE SAME LINE AS FAULT (3): line 223 carries nine bold marks where the bold spans on that line come to eight, so one of the nine is unpaired, and that one unpaired mark is the entire odd count in that file.** So faults (3) and (4) are one line and one pair of characters, and both are here at the length they need, and both are routed to a Volume 06 review repair pass and not repaired here, because the file is a live state file that a writer reads first and a repair pass that guesses which of the nine marks on line 223 is the stray one has made a worse file than the one it was sent to fix. The nineteen stray closing quotation marks in Chapters 0281 to 0290 are the fifth and are already routed.

## DECISIONS OF RECORD, VOLUME 09 — taken by the Volume 08 close phase, in its own words

**Four decisions were open when Volume 08 was closed and none of them was decided by any batch, because a batch is not allowed to decide. The Volume 06 outline phase took four. The Volume 07 outline phase took four. The Volume 07 close phase took four. The Volume 08 outline phase took four more and amended the one paragraph the close phase left to it. This close phase is the fifth phase in this repository that has been permitted to decide, and it took four more, on the date in the phase ledger for `workspace/volume-08/close`. They are written here, in the series outline, so that a reader of this document in a year can see what was decided, when, and what it cost. Nothing in this file moved that was not named below, the ending did not move, no new final enemy was introduced, and the antagonist ladder and the relationship milestones are untouched. This block sits after the block above and before the volume list, and a reader who wants the series and not the argument should skip all four blocks. There is no Volume 09 entry in the volume list and that is deliberate: an entry there is a line, and a line is the Volume 09 outline phase's to write and to audit, and this phase has audited one and has not adopted it.**

**DECISION ONE — THE VOICE. CONFIRMED, AND IT CARRIES SIX THINGS THIS VOLUME FOUND, FOUR OF THEM ABOUT INSTRUMENTS AND NOT ABOUT PROSE.**

**Volume 06's voice is this book's voice and it is confirmed for the rest of the run. Fifty-day volumes, one chapter to one day, about a page and a yard and a wage in salt. Nobody gets stronger. The antagonist is a method and not a form, and Volume 08 proved the second half of that in a room with a date on the door: the worst thing anybody in this flood does to anybody is write a name at the top of a piece of paper. The only instrument this book has that a return cannot be filled in from is a blank. There is no narrator frame, and the narrator may not say *this chapter*, *this volume*, *in this batch* or *the reader*, and Volume 02's thirty-one uses of *this volume* are named as the one place in the manuscript where a narrator may say it and nowhere else. The range of *fifty-day volumes* is from here and is not a rule about the past: Volume 04 is the recorded exception and is not reopened, and a volume after that which wants a double day has to argue for it in its own outline against a rule that is now the default and may not quietly ignore it.**

**What Volume 08 adds is six rules, they bind every batch after this line, and four of them are about instruments and not about prose, which is the single most useful thing this volume has for the writer who comes after it.**

1. **THE SHARE OF WORDS INSIDE BOLD MARKS IS A SYMPTOM AND NOT A TARGET, and a card that sets a target for it is setting a target for how many people a chapter is allowed to have.** Measured on Volume 08's fifty files on the published definition, **the range is 5.11 to 24.72 and the mean is 12.39 unweighted and 12.38 weighted by words, on 87,216 words.** The two ends of Batch 0005's published range are the two ends of the two subjects and not of two qualities: **5.39 in Chapter 0400 is objects and a road and nobody talking, and 17.90 in Chapter 0392 is a woman on a bench being told that a name she has not given away is going out of the city.** The two lowest chapters of the fifty are a roof down by hand in a squall and three posts going into the ground. **The mean is a consequence and not a plan, no volume may read its own mean as a quality figure, and no review may certify or fail a volume on one.**
2. **TWO INSTRUMENTS THAT BOTH RETURN A NUMBER A WRITER EXPECTED ARE NOT TWO INSTRUMENTS, AND A FIGURE AT ZERO DESERVES THE SAME SUSPICION AS A FIGURE AT TWELVE.** Two chapters of Volume 08 came out of their first pass with **no speech in them at all**: the bolded instrument read them as a legitimate low figure at 0.00 and 0.00, the exchange instrument read them as a legitimate zero, **and it took running the second instrument to see the first.** Both numbers were correct and the fault was invisible to both. **So: whenever two instruments on the same ten files return a number the writer expected, run the third one before believing either, and a zero gets read twice.**
3. **THE CHORUS FRAME IS A SENTENCE AND NOT A TOKEN, ITS REPAIR IS A HAND JOB, AND FOUR WRITTEN WARNINGS DID NOT REMOVE THE HABIT, SO THE RULE HAS TO BE A RULE AND NOT A WARNING.** Volume 08's last batch came out of its first pass at one instance in every 4.4 paragraphs, which is exactly Batch 0004's first-pass figure and exactly the density the Batch 0003 review named as a fault. **A writer told about a frame four times wrote it twice at the density the review named, and the telling is not the instrument.** The rule, and it is a rule about sentences: **a chorus frame may carry an observation into a paragraph and may not restate one the paragraph has already made; where the observation is new it is written as plain narration in the narrator's own voice and the frame is not used at all; where the frame is kept it is kept because the room's not-noticing is the subject, and it appears once in a scene and not twice.** No figure is set, and a record that publishes a count of this frame and calls it flat while the frame is ending paragraphs is reporting its own failure as a result. **The line 1.9 → 6.2, 4.4 → 22.3, 4.4 → 16.6 across the last three batches is not a trend and must not be published as one: the first figure of each pair is a first pass and the second is a hand repair. Volume 07 is the standing debt at 11.3 instances a chapter against Volume 06's 0.18, and a repair pass on a finished volume is a restart, so it is carried and not touched, and the remedy for the going-forward case is this rule and not a repair of Volume 07.**
4. **A NAME CAN GO AT THE TOP OF A PAGE AND NOT BE PRINTED IN THE REPOSITORY THAT PRINTED THE PAGE. Volume 08 did that once, and it was a decision and not an accident, and the close confirms it in its own words.** A woman said a name out loud in daylight in a room with a date on the door in front of about nine people, and four days later a page went out with that name at the top of it, and **the name is on no file in this repository: not in the two chapters, not in the batch record, not in the bible, not in the series outline, not in the state layer and not in the close record, which is the one page a writer reads first.** The reason is the volume's own: the antagonist is a method, the method is a name at the top of a piece of paper, **and a repository that printed the name would be doing the thing the volume is about.** What it costs is one reader's advantage and every other reader's protection; what it buys is that a page in this book costs something to produce and a file in this repository is not a page. **A later phase may reverse it and may not do it silently, and the same protection already holds the volume's own name, which is a man's answer spoken once in Chapter 0357 and is in no file, and it held through fifty chapters and a close record that had every reason to print it and did not.**
5. **PUBLISH THE INSTRUMENT OR PUBLISH NOTHING, as a rule about every figure and not only about figures about objects.** Volume 08's last batch printed sixteen object counts and no marker list, said in a sentence that the markers were published beside the table, **and seven of the sixteen did not survive being re-measured on markers that are now written down.** All sixteen marker sets are now in the third column of that batch record's §4 and twelve of the fourteen surviving figures reproduce exactly. A count whose instrument is not published is not a measurement, and a record that says its instrument is published when it is not is worse than a wrong figure, because it teaches the reader to trust it.
6. **THE HOUSE HAS TWO CHORUS INSTRUMENTS IN CIRCULATION UNDER ONE NAME, AND THEY DIFFER BY A CASE FLAG THAT NOBODY PUBLISHED.** On the case-sensitive string *about four people in*, Volume 08's fifty files stand at **158 in 1,215 paragraphs, one in 7.7**; on the case-insensitive run of the same search, **204 in the same 1,215, one in 6.0**; and on the variant-agnostic head *about four people* plus any word, **266 case-sensitive and 347 case-insensitive.** The paragraph denominators reproduce exactly in all five batch records, **and Batch 0001 published 94 on the bare head and the case-sensitive instrument gives 94 chapter by chapter, while Batch 0002 withdrew a figure of forty-seven as being on no reading of its own instrument and said the files give sixty-seven — forty-seven is the case-sensitive figure and sixty-seven is the case-insensitive one, so that withdrawal was a case-sensitivity error — and Batch 0003 and Batch 0005 published from the case-insensitive run while Batch 0004 published the case-sensitive pair.** **A difference of a case flag moved a published count by up to twenty-nine instances in a batch of ten and was invisible to everybody, and the general form of the rule is decision five: two instruments that differ by anything are two instruments, and an instrument is not named until its flags are.**

**What it costs, said plainly and not softened. The series keeps its voice and gets four instruments rules and one protection it did not have, and it loses the right to treat a mean as a result, a zero as a fact about a chapter, a warning as an instrument, a published count without its instrument as a measurement, and a repository as a place where a name spent on a page can be looked up. The only thing this decision takes away is the convenience of the person reading a file, and that convenience is the thing that has been costing this manuscript a review finding for four volumes.**

**DECISION TWO — THE LINE. AUDITED ON THE CHAPTER FILES HERE, AND NOT ADOPTED HERE, because a line is a plan and a close records a volume and does not plan the next one.**

**The standing test is at `outline/volume-07.md` §19 and it is the one that matters: a line is made of words the manuscript has never used AND of things the manuscript has never seen, and a line that is only the first collides with a page that already means something else. Volume 08's incoming line was struck and replaced in one act by the Volume 07 close phase, four of its nouns collided, and the audit that caught them is the audit run here.**

**THE INSTRUMENT, PUBLISHED BESIDE EVERY FIGURE, because a block that publishes a figure and not the thing that produced it is a claim and not a count: every figure below is a word-bounded count, the token bounded by whitespace, punctuation or a line end, case-sensitive unless the figure says otherwise, taken over whole files and not line by line, and THE COUNT WAS TAKEN OVER 400 CHAPTER FILES, and 400 is the number, and there are fifty files in each of the eight volumes.**

**AT ZERO ACROSS ALL 400 CHAPTER FILES, word-bounded and case-sensitive, and still available to a line: *Sundering*, *constitution*, *template*, *templates*, *charters*, *Redroot Homes*, *caravan*, *convoy*, *itinerary*, *waybill*, *Commonwealth*, *commonwealth*, *filler*, *march*, *Earthsiders*, *isekai*, *System*, *sixth month*, *of next month*, *of this new month*, *this chapter*, *in this batch*, and — measured here for the first time and published because a plural of the volume's own last image is worth knowing — *fences*, *boundaries*, *journeys*, *orchards*, *hundreds*, *tolls*, *warrants*, *levies*, *inquest*, *coroner*, *bailiff*, *reeve*, *ledgers* and *huts*.** Twenty-one inherited zeros reproduce exactly on 400 files, and *labor* is at zero while *labour* stands at 55 in 36, which is the spelling the record above already corrected once.

**AND THE LINE'S OWN LOAD-BEARING NOUNS ARE NOT IN THAT LIST, and this is the finding and it is the same finding that made Volume 08's line work, and it is published here so that the next phase inherits the reason and not only the rule.** *Man* is 6,612 in 398 files, *thing* 5,176 in 400, *asked* 4,535 in 398, *hundred* 3,873 in 392, *person* 3,246 in 387, *page* 3,028 in 370, *road* 1,987 in 333, *column* 1,052 in 239, *form* 832 in 207, *roll* 527 in 172, *fence* 124 in 23, *boundary* 127 in 54, *boundary*'s plural at zero. **A line built on a noun at zero is a line about a thing this world has never seen, and the four volumes that did that are the four volumes that produced a series outline one volume behind. A line built on a noun in the high column is a line about the oldest thing in this book, and Volume 08 is the first volume to say so out loud: a form with no column in it is not a new kind of thing, it is the oldest thing there is.** **AND THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS SENTENCE PRINTED 6,613 AND 5,177 AND 3,247 AND 1,988 FOR THOSE FOUR NOUNS, each one a single instance high, every file denominator in the row right, and all four are corrected here on a re-run of the instrument printed above them. The correction is published rather than made silently, because the fifth of the four decisions this block takes says a count whose instrument is not published is not a measurement, and a block that breaks its own rule two paragraphs after stating it is the fault the rule exists for. The other nine figures in the row reproduce exactly and stand.**

**THE CANDIDATE THIS PHASE AUDITED, in one sentence, and it is a candidate and not an adoption: *a boundary has to be agreed by the two men who stand on each side of it and neither of them is going to be asked, and the only instrument this world has for a boundary is a page four hundred miles away that three people are walking towards, and there is no form in four hundred miles for saying where a thing somebody else built begins.*** Its load-bearing nouns are *boundary*, *man*, *page*, *form*, *road*, *person* and *asked*, and **every one of them is in the high column of this manuscript's own vocabulary, and that is not a mark against it: it is the same result Volume 08's line produced and the reason that line worked.** The second half of the test therefore has to be carried by the things and not by the words, and the three things this candidate needs are named here so that a writer can check them: **a fence that is finished and was built by somebody nobody sent; a man who walks a four-hundred-mile road and has seen three people on it in nine years; and a question that went to a room on day 396 and was answered by nobody, about four of the nine having an answer and about four of them saying nothing.**

**AND THE ONE COLLISION THE AUDIT FOUND, which is the finding that decides the shape of the next line more than any count: the question is spent as a line.** *Who is a fence for* is on a page in Chapter 0396 and is unanswered, and a Volume 09 line that is that question again is Volume 08 with the nouns changed, exactly as `outline/volume-08.md` §18 forbids. **So the next line has to be built on the standing the unanswered question left and not on the question itself**, which is the standing: two men in one room who were not asked, a man who cannot move a thing and has not tried, three people on a road that nobody in this city can be shown one person on, and a boundary that has two accounts in this city and no name on any page in the world to ask about. **The Volume 09 outline phase adopts, strikes or replaces this candidate in its own words, and whatever it adopts it publishes the same instrument and the same count.**

**DECISION THREE — THE CALENDAR. DAYS 401 TO 450, ONE CHAPTER TO ONE DAY, THE FORM UNCHANGED, AND THE BARE MONTH DOES NOT END.**

**The Bare Month has no stated length, by a notary's refusal on a page in Chapter 0316, and it did not end inside Volume 08 and no chapter of that volume ended it. So the next volume begins on an unknown number of days into a month of no stated length, and that is settled here deliberately and not left to happen.**

**Volume 09 is days 401 to 450, one chapter to one day, no double day, and the form is *the Nth day of the Bare Month*, the Nth being day less 315, which is the sixth form and is the one registered at `bible/power-system.md` §33A item 2 and named on a page in Chapter 0316 and in no other chapter and in no other file. Day 401 is the eighty-sixth day of the Bare Month and a Wednesday, because day 400 is a Tuesday; day 450 is the hundred and thirty-fifth and a Wednesday. All fifty rows are derived from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else, and they are at `bible/power-system.md` §51, which this phase wrote, and they were validated before they were published by reproducing §33B's fifty rows for days 351 to 400 out of the same two founding figures at zero mismatches. No batch may make, amend or extend any of it.**

**AND THE DECISION INSIDE THE DECISION, and it is the same one the last two close phases took and for the same reason: THE BARE MONTH DOES NOT END INSIDE VOLUME 09, AND THIS SECTION GIVES IT NO LENGTH AND SAYS SO IN A SENTENCE.** A chapter of Volume 09 may write an ordinal for any day of it from the eighty-sixth to the hundred and thirty-fifth. **No chapter may write a day after the hundred and thirty-fifth, may not say the month is nearly over, may not say what it is a month of, may not convert a count into a span, may not print *a hundred and thirty-five days* or *four months* or *since the month began* as a figure, and may not say what the month does at its end, how many days it has, or whether it is long or short. The words *length*, *long* and *short* applied to the month stay at zero.** The only two instruments a chapter has for a date are a count of days of this flood and a day of the Bare Month with its own form, and the second is the cheapest date in this book and the only one nobody has to check twice.

**And the rule for whoever comes after, unchanged in substance and restated because it is the point of deciding it here: a seventh form exists the moment the end of the Bare Month is on a page, and it comes onto a page the same way the sixth one did — a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, by a person, who says the cost out loud first and is refused by somebody before she does it, and who then declines to say how long the new month is and says why. The Short Month and the Bare Month were both made that way, in Chapter 0256 and in Chapter 0316, by a notary of fifty who named one and then named the other for the same reason. A calendar is not a decision an outline takes and a batch guesses. It is a decision a person makes in a room, and every form in this book is one, including the ones in the bible.** If a later volume needs the seventh form and has not got the end of the Bare Month on a page, it writes inside the Bare Month and **it may not declare the month over.**

**What it costs. The series gets a calendar it can plan fifty days against, and it pays for it with a seventh form that still does not exist: Volume 09 is a volume that begins on an unknown number of days into a month and ends on another one, and the protection that buys is that no volume can inherit an end by accident. A hundred and thirty-five days of a month of no stated length is the longest stretch any volume in this series has spent inside one month, and the alternative was to give the month a length in a bible file, which is the fault this repository has been repairing for four volumes, or in a chapter that guesses, which §33A item 2 forbids.**

**DECISION FOUR — THE CAST, THE INSTRUMENT, THE POOL, AND TWO DEBTS CARRIED DELIBERATELY.**

**No name and no place arrives by narration in this book. It is checked for a room, in daylight, with a date on it, by a person. Volume 06's state layer broke it by naming a gatekeeper who was on no page and a review caught it, and the name is in the repository only as the record of the strike. The instrument is the oldest one in the book and it is unchanged: a document arrives and is read out, and the four days of notice is what brings people to the room, and a notice on any other day is a three-day instrument and a three-day instrument does not fail, it arrives warm.**

**SO: Volume 09's first instrument is a document nobody sent for, read out in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, in the local language, in front of about nine people, and what is read out is what puts the cast on a page.** Volume 08's document was a return with four places under a heading and no place for the person, and the reading put nine people in a room over a market on a page and cost a household its fourth place. **Volume 09's document is not chosen by this phase and must not be, because choosing it is the outline's first act; what this phase decides is the instrument and the discipline, and the standing rules about it, which are these: no name at the top of anything a notice is written on; four days between a notice and the room; the notice to a place and not to a house; a document is not a mystery and is not a puzzle; a document that arrives is read out and not solved; and the person who reads it out says out loud what it is for before anybody asks.**

**THE POOL, measured, with the definition published because a descriptor map counts occurrences and does not decide which occurrence is the same person, and that decision is a decision and belongs to an outline or a close phase and is taken there and not in a chapter. Definition: every occurrence at sentence level of the word *man*, *woman*, *person*, *girl* or *boy*, followed by *of*, *about* and a spelled-out number, bounded by whitespace, punctuation or a line end, on a file with the emphasis marks removed and the whitespace collapsed. The instrument validates itself on Volume 07's fifty files, where it reproduces `outline/volume-08.md` §20.8's published map row for row, all twenty-nine rows, before it is used on Volume 08's fifty.**

**Twelve descriptors that stood in Volume 07's fifty are at zero in Volume 08's fifty, and those are the pool a new person may be taken from, and the 400-file figure is printed beside each because a low 400-file figure is the best guard this repository has against a merge nobody can see: *woman of about twenty-four* 112, *man of about thirty-six* 59, *woman of about thirty-three* 34, *woman of about sixty-one* 27, *boy of about eleven* 13, *woman of about twenty-eight* 11, *woman of about sixty* 8, *man of about sixty-three* 6, *man of about sixty-four* 4, *person of about twenty-one* 2, *man of about thirty-three* 2, *man of about twenty-one* 1. **AND THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS SENTENCE SAID NINETEEN, WHICH IS A DIFFERENT FIGURE AND A TRUE ONE ABOUT A DIFFERENT THING: nineteen is the number of distinct descriptors standing ON Volume 08's fifty files, and twelve is the number of Volume 07's that are at zero in them, and the two are not interchangeable, and a pool that is described with the count of the crowd in it is a pool a writer will draw a returning person out of.** All twelve figures below reproduce exactly on 400 files and the twelve listed are the twelve measured. And the four that are crowded and may not be used for a new person: *woman of about thirty-four* 362 in 147, *man of about thirty-four* 341 in 139, *man of about forty-four* 162 in 85, *man of about thirty-nine* 177 in 93. **AND THE FIRST EDITION OF THAT HALF-SENTENCE PRINTED 351 in 142, 283 in 108, 162 in 85 and 133 in 69, AND THE FAULT WAS NOT ARITHMETIC AND NOT A CARELESS COPY, IT WAS A DENOMINATOR: all four of the first edition's figures reproduce EXACTLY on the 350 chapter files of Volumes 01 to 07, where `outline/volume-08.md` §20.8 correctly labels them the 350-file map's crowded four, and the close phase carried them into this block and printed them as 400-file figures beside a sentence about what a 400-file figure is for. A count that is true and attached to the wrong denominator is the same fault as a count that is wrong, and it is the worse of the two, because a reader who re-runs the instrument cannot reproduce the published number and concludes the instrument is broken.** The three that moved are corrected above; *man of about forty-four* stands at 162 in 85 on both denominators and it is the coincidence that hid this for a phase — **one figure in four reproduced by accident is what makes a denominator swap survive a review.** The withdrawal is published rather than made silently, and `outline/volume-08.md` §20.8 is NOT at fault and must not be amended: its four figures are right and they are about 350 files. **And the one that is not available to anybody at all: *man of about thirty-seven*, which is at zero in Volumes 07 and 08 and belongs to a real person on a page in `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0293.md` who is a question-carrier and is not the board-trader, and a chapter may not give him a trade, a board, a market or a refusal.**

**AND THE THING THIS VOLUME FOUND ABOUT THE PROTAGONIST, which is a decision and not a repair, and it is the one finding of the two the reviews raised that a close is allowed to settle. Adrian Vale is named in SEVEN of Volume 08's fifty chapters — 0352, 0355, 0360, 0371, 0376, 0381 and 0392 — and in no chapter in any form in the other forty-three, and the seven are the plan's own list and not a batch's doing, and Chapter 0392 is the last of them. He is Stage 2 on all fifty days, performed no working, opened no threshold, is not offered a workway and asks for none, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the fifty files.** The rule that produced seven is that he is named in the chapters in which he is reading a page and in no chapter in which people decline to ask him, **and the finding is that the rule is a rule and not a law, and that a volume whose subject is a method has nothing to give him, because the only person in this manuscript who cannot be put on a page by a method is him.** **SO: Volume 09's plan must include him in at least one chapter in which he reads nothing. The number of chapters he is in is the outline's to set and this phase does not set it, no chapter may be opened or moved to make room for one, and no chapter of Volume 09 may get him stronger, perform a working, open a threshold, name the other world, or be offered a workway he accepts.**

**AND THE TWO DESCRIPTOR DEBTS THIS VOLUME LEFT, both of which this phase is CARRYING, and says so, because a close record that carries a debt silently is a record nobody can check.** *(1) The figure **about two hundred rooms** is on the page in nine chapters of Volume 08 — ten occurrences, measured on the files, in 0352 three times, 0360, 0367 twice, 0369, 0374 and 0375 — and it belongs on the pages as they stand to Adrian Vale in narration, to a woman who keeps a sheet in an apron in a mouth, to the man of about forty-eight twice in a mouth, to the man of about thirty-one in narration, and to one speech paragraph in Chapter 0374 that carries no descriptor at all. **The state layer says the figure is at zero in all fifty chapters and that is a false zero, and a false zero in the state layer is the most expensive kind of fault in this repository, because a writer inherits it as permission.** The batch records describe three men; the files show at least five people and a woman. **This phase settles the rule and not the pages: the figure is not available to a new person, no chapter of Volume 09 may give it to anybody, and the descriptor map for Volume 09 must record that five people already hold it — and the attribution itself stays on the list, unpaid, for a review repair pass and not for a writer, because deciding which of five people on nine pages of a finished volume a figure belongs to is a repair and not a decision a close makes at the end of a run.** *(2) The man of about fifty-seven was in ten rooms in about a fortnight and was asked one question in none of them, and no chapter of the volume prints a room count for anybody, and his counter is on a page at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0364.md:7` and not in a plan, and a figure that goes down from ten to nine over three days is a subtraction taken across the wrong boundary — which is the fault a batch prompt made and a batch corrected, and it is the standing reason no card sets a room count for anybody. **REPAIRED BY THE VOLUME 09 REVIEW REPAIR PASS, NAMED IN PLACE: this item gave him *eleven* rooms and pointed at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0390.md:35` for it, and that page is the man of about thirty-nine, whose own counts are eleven at `:35` and ten at `chapter-0388.md:33`. The fifty-seven's is ten, at `chapter-0364.md:7`, and Volume 07's close gives him nine, at `state/volume-07-close.md:73`. Two men, two counts, and neither man holds the other's.***

**What it costs, stated plainly. The series gains a cast rule it can be held to, a pool measured rather than remembered, an instrument that is the oldest one in the book, and a decision about the protagonist that is a decision. It loses the convenience of a number of chapters for the man who is the reason this book exists, and it keeps a debt about a room count that a state file says is not there, and it keeps the standing prohibition on the man of about thirty-seven, which is the only person in this repository whose descriptor is protected by a decision nobody has yet had to make twice.**

**AND WHAT THIS PHASE COULD NOT DECIDE, which is the same answer the last two close phases gave and it is the answer that has now protected this book three times. The Bare Month does not end inside Volume 09, and this phase did not decide how long it is and cannot.** A volume that ended it on a chapter boundary would print the end of a month whose length a notary of fifty refused on a page, in the second volume to inherit that refusal, and it would have to do it in one of two ways: by a sentence in a state file, which is the thing this repository has been repairing for four volumes, or by a chapter that guesses, which §33A item 2 forbids. **So Volume 09 writes the eighty-sixth to the hundred and thirty-fifth day of the Bare Month and stops, and a seventh form exists the moment the end of it is on a page, in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, by a person, who says the cost out loud first and is refused before she does it.**

**AND THE FIVE DEBTS, of which four are older than this volume, all re-measured on the files by this phase and all still unpaid at the same line numbers: nineteen stray closing quotation marks in Chapters 0281 to 0290; `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0341.md:149`, which says *the word on the slate has sixteen days left on it* where the arithmetic is 342 − 341 = 1; a near-duplicate across the 0254 and 0262 boundary, measured at a thirty-nine-word run on this phase's instrument; the unmarked first-person paragraph at `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0175.md:47`; and *about two hundred rooms* on five people and a woman. All five are routed to a review repair pass and not to a writer, and a phase that quietly takes a routed item to save itself a line is how nineteen quotation marks became a four-volume debt.** Two more were found by this phase in files it did not own and are routed the same way: **three sentences in Chapters 0364 and 0396 that put the shut road above or at the top of this city where the plan's own resolution and two repaired chapters put it out of this city, one of the three being a woman's speech in a room with a date on the door; and a published instrument in `bible/power-system.md` §50 item 9 whose two figures are Batch 0005's ten files and not the fifty and reads as a conflict to anybody who compares it with the close record.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE, BY PATH: `workspace/volume-09/outline/PROMPT.md`, and it is a VOLUME 09 OUTLINE PHASE and not a batch. It writes `outline/volume-09.md`, the first Volume 09 batch's cards and no prose, and it decides the line, the cast, the calendar's shape inside §51's rules, and the number of chapters Adrian Vale is in — and it audits the line before it adopts it, publishes the instrument with every figure, and writes no chapter.**


## Volume progression

### Volume 01: The First Door (Chapters 1–50)

- **Central pressure:** A damaged Anchorstone pulls a **breach** open in the Lock of Salt gate at Saltwake. Adrian must survive, stop the damage from spreading past the breach, and decide whether any controlled crossing is safer than an uncontrolled one.
- **Immediate promise:** The first ten chapters establish the real world, the first cohort, the no-respawn rule, the first Witness working, and Ivenn’s first altered prompt.
- **Early escalation (Chapters 4–5):** Adrian’s unplanned repair of the lock pushes water and pressure out to a joint beside the outer channel that nobody is watching; that joint opens as the **outer tear** and drags six other Earthside people through. Nobody consented and nobody was holding the door, so this is a batch-level forced expansion rather than a failure of procedure. The six are strangers to each other and to Adrian, not a recruited test group. It is the midpoint of Chapters 1–10 only.
- **Midpoint reversal (Chapter 25):** a *controlled, locally witnessed* attempt to stabilize the lock succeeds physically and still pulls in a second, unrequested group of arrivals. The System is not a single-player interface, the locals are not waiting safely outside a tutorial, and — the worse news — a properly agreed opening can fail too. Do not reuse the Chapter 4–5 mechanism here; there are witnesses and a cost in Chapter 25.
- **Late cost (Chapter 38):** Owen Park is killed during a bad crossing and his return mark becomes the first black scar carried in a person — a black scar in a place at the outer threshold and a black line on the chapel stone came first.
- **Climax (Chapters 41–50):** Adrian joins a collective working to close the dangerous main breach, keeps only a narrow quorum route, and publicly accepts responsibility for the next test. The death is the price the volume extracts; the working is the climax, and he does not get it back by becoming stronger.
- **Concrete resolution:** Saltwake and the survivors sign a trial license. The first players have shelter, medical care, and a route by which a return mark may be established after review, but no land or template privileges.
- **Next-volume question:** If the first bridge can be made to work, who gets to decide what the second group is allowed to do?

### Volume 02: Salt and Silver (Chapters 51–100)

- **Central pressure:** The first cohort must survive a winter of local politics, scarce materials, and competing definitions of “welcome.”
- **Midpoint:** A player uses a workway to seize a shrine, and Adrian’s promise of fair access turns into a public liability.
- **Climax:** Ivenn engineers a false oath that would make the Lantern Concord permanently own the License. Adrian voids it by taking on a local debt and loses an easy route home.
- **Resolution:** The first mixed Wayhouse and Open Hand exchange are established under local law.
- **Question:** Why do Crown records describe earlier Bright Pilgrims when no living local can explain where they went?

### Volume 03: The Crown’s Ledger (Chapters 101–150)

- **Central pressure:** Aurel’s tribunal decides whether Earthside arrivals are guests, gods, refugees, or an occupation.
- **Midpoint:** Adrian’s ability to translate Crown Law is used to prove that a local village “consented” to a seizure it never understood.
- **Climax:** Adrian refuses to become a Crown-appointed interpreter with unlimited authority. The public hearing records the players as named guests under a revocable compact.
- **Resolution:** The Glass Crown recognizes the bridge provisionally and sends a delegation to the First Landing ruins.
- **Question:** What happened to the people whose names were removed from the first records?

### Volume 04: The White Ledger (Chapters 151–200)

- **Central pressure:** Jonah Pike establishes a successful, well-run player settlement that takes land, water, and template access without local consent.
- **Midpoint:** Adrian’s delayed response causes a local family to lose a water gate. He cannot solve the problem simply by declaring the settlement illegal.
- **Climax:** Adrian closes a profitable resource crossing and helps the Reed Commons recover the water gate, splitting the player coalition.
- **Resolution:** Redroot Commons and the Reed Commons establish a land-and-labor compact; Jonah becomes a serious rival.
- **Question:** How many people on Earth are disappearing, and who is exploiting the fear?

### Volume 05: An Island Of Boards (Chapters 201–250) — RECONCILED. Published as written; the plan of record was struck.

**The five beats this file carried for this volume were never started and have been struck by the decision recorded above. What is below is what the volume is, as `state/volume-05-close.md` states it, and it is a summary of fifty chapters and not a plan for chapters that do not exist.**

- **Central pressure, as written:** a posting of nine lines, bound to a compact of eleven, goes up on a wall of a store nine miles up the upper road on day 201, and a queue — the only functioning way out of this world — is the ninth line of the posting, and the posting cannot be administered, and a right of refusal on it cannot be afforded, and about four hundred and forty people on eleven miles of flats are paid in salt for a buyer nine hundred miles away that no route in this world reaches.
- **What it answered:** the posting lost its date on about day 230 and then eight of its nine lines between day 231 and day 244 and kept the ninth, because it was written last with a harder piece of chalk by a hand that leaned on it. **Nobody can now say whether the ninth works, the man who wrote it has refused five times to say, and the man who washes the board nine inches below it reads it every morning and has never said one word about it.** A sack of salt became nine feet of ground in a working yard that nobody walks on. A rope went over a bank and came back up tied by a man who does not own it. Two brooms sit on some boards nine feet from a keeper of a gate and nobody will say who brought the second. A man put his own coat over a pan and did not turn the pan out. A man came eleven miles to ask a question and did not ask it.
- **What it did not answer, on purpose:** whether the ninth line works; what a day of the trestle count is; the two holes nine feet apart that may not be joined; the about four hundred and forty; a girl of seventeen who cannot be fetched; and the return crisis the struck line was going to pay, which is now a recorded debt and is owed.
- **Its cost:** nobody got stronger. A page fell apart.
- **Question it actually raised, and the volume line's question is now owed:** **a line that makes a person findable and cannot be administered or counted is a border's working definition.** That is the border question, argued for fifty days in yards and settled by nobody, and Volume 06 is built on it.

### Volume 06: The Hearthguard (Chapters 251–300) — the volume's own name on a page is *The Days On A Gate*

- **Series title, kept:** *The Hearthguard*, because the Hearthguard is the body at the centre of the volume and because `outline/ending.md` names Hearthguard chapters. **The volume's own name, chosen on a page, is *The Days On A Gate*, and no chapter states that sentence in narration.**
- **Central pressure:** a Crown instrument declaring an emergency in the crossing and the ground adjoining it comes up the river and requires the days on every gate between this city and the Hearth Marches to be entered in a schedule, with a date, a gate, a holder and the days — **and a gate is not owned, and what a household has of one is the days on it, and a household can only lose them by agreeing, and this instrument is not asking.**
- **Midpoint:** Adrian's plan fails because the only man who can refuse it on the Hearthguard's behalf refuses it. **The safe route is safe for the road and takes eleven weeks of drying from three villages, and the refusal is four sentences in a room in daylight with a date on it, and it is correct, and it costs the season.**
- **Climax:** the days go back to nine households in one day, in daylight, on nine flats and nine kitchens, unasked of anybody — and the road north of the Marches closes and does not open again in this flood. **No threshold is opened, no working is performed, and the passage privilege is not spent; the cost is a route that is gone and about four hundred and forty people who lose a season.**
- **Resolution:** a regional ceasefire that nobody signed, administered by a body that floats being poled to nine places on nine days, holding because nine households were heard. **The bridge is local and contested because the one instrument in four hundred miles that cannot be entered in a schedule is a queue, and a man who wrote it on purpose will not say whether it works.** The first Sundering scar becomes visible beneath the Marches — a straight line in the ground, seen from a gate, by a person doing her work, **not measured, not dug, and not by anybody with a rod.**
- **Question:** what was the old Crown trying to keep in one version? **This volume earns it by showing that the Marches keep a roll of gates and the Crown has sent a roll of people, and that this is the second roll of the same gates this city has ever had, and that nobody has ever put the two side by side.** It does not answer it and Volume 07 must not be told the answer.
- **What this line is paying that the struck Volume 05 line was not:** *Regent Solenne Dary* is spoken in a room in Chapter 0254 and is not a discovery; a Hearthguard commander is on a page from Chapter 0008 and is one of the four witnesses of Chapter 0010 and is recorded in `bible/characters.md` as the man who **can refuse the route on the Hearthguard's behalf**; the Marches are a place west of this city that a roadreader can walk to; *Sundering* is the name of a thing three hundred pages old that nobody has used since Volume 01. **Four of the six things this line needs were already on a page in this book and none of them was used for four volumes.**

### Volume 07: A Word For The Line (Chapters 301–350) — RECONCILED. The line this file carried for this volume was struck and replaced; what is below is what the volume is, as `outline/volume-07.md` states it.

**The five beats this file carried for this volume were never started and have been struck by the decision recorded above, in the same act and the same words as the Volume 05 line was struck: an expedition to the First Landing and the Steppe Anchorstones becomes trapped by overlapping versions of roads, weather and memory; a System quest labels a living Veyran settlement "unwritten" and rewards Adrian for erasing a false record and he realises the prompt is being edited; Adrian restores a disputed river boundary by accepting a permanent scar instead of forcing the old version and Tamsin leads the route decision and he follows it; the team confirms the First Sundering was caused by forced single-version rule rather than by a monster or a natural disaster; and the question is why the Quiet Choir has spent centuries making a singular device singular.** What is below is a plan for fifty chapters and not a summary of chapters that do not exist.

- **Central pressure:** a boundary on a lane past two gates has to be settled before the season turns, and the only page in four hundred miles that says where the line runs is a copy of an entry in a public register in a town four hundred miles inland, made by a clerk about forty years ago and copied from an entry a hundred and forty years old, and **nobody in this city can go and look at the page the copy was made from.** The only instrument this world has for reaching a page that is not to hand is four people and a question, and they have not left in a hundred and fifty-six days, their only instruction is *to open nothing*, and **nobody has ever asked any of the four whether it will go.**
- **The line in the ground, and the word for it, and this is the volume's real inheritance:** about a foot wide and about two inches deep, from the near end of a row of trees to a stone nobody put there, about nine hundred paces off a shut gate, **not measured, not dug, not counted, not probed.** It is called a scar in a room, in daylight, with a date on it, by a person, in Chapter 0310, and **the word is taken back off the only page it is ever on in Chapter 0342, in chalk, with a cloth**, because a name is only a removal when the thing named can be found, and a line in an orchard cannot be found until somebody starts going to it.
- **Midpoint, day 325, a Thursday:** Adrian's plan is to send somebody four hundred miles for the page; it is correct about the facts and worse about the cost, and **Tamsin Quill leads the decision that nobody goes, and he follows it, and he works it out in a yard standing beside the man who digs, who tells him nothing and has not been asked anything, and the chapter says he worked it out and does not say he was right.** This is relationship milestone six, paid here and not deferred to Volume 08.
- **Climax, day 342, a Sunday:** the word is taken back off a slate in chalk, in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, by the man of about thirty-four who wrote the ninth line, who is not the protagonist. **No working, no threshold, no panel, no stage, and the passage privilege is not spent and not named. The climax is a subtraction: after it the line has no word on any page in the world, the reader has been told what it is called, no page can say so, and the protection the naming bought is gone with the name.**
- **Resolution:** the boundary is **not settled.** It is entered in a register as a line that may not be dug, with a day on it and nothing under the day and no word on it, and a man who paid money for half a holding is told what he has, and is angry, and is right. Three of the four said yes in their own words and one said no in a sentence that is hers, and a party of four is four, and the page is still four hundred miles away. The Crown's schedule stands, is correct, and is neither filled in nor withdrawn. The road north of the Marches is shut on day 350 for fifty-six days and nobody shut it. **The last image is a fence four feet off a line nobody has measured, put by a man nobody sent, on a Monday, and neither party ever agreed where the line was and the fence does not care.**
- **What the volume answers, and it is the first motive this book has answered and it is not a monster:** a Crown a hundred and forty years ago had nineteen stones and a page that did not agree, and a boundary with two accounts of it is a boundary somebody has to fight over, so a court made the accounts agree. It worked. Eight names were moved and four people were buried under a stone that was not theirs, and the man who keeps the roll of the dead of that gate has sent the count to an office four times a year for nineteen years, and a clerk wrote eight in a book, and nobody came. **The reason the old Crown was right and the reason it was wrong are the same sentence, and the modern Crown is doing the same thing with a column for a holder, and this volume says so once, in a mouth, and does not say it twice.**
- **The volume's finding, and it is about a name and not about history:** *a name is only a removal when the thing named can be found, and a line in an orchard cannot be found because nobody is going to it, and so a line can be named safely, and the moment somebody starts going to it the name becomes a description and the name has to go.* **That is the first time this book has asked what a page is FOR rather than what a page does, and it is asked about a line in an orchard because that is the one thing in four hundred miles the day-154 sentence does not fit — and that sentence is not said again anywhere in this volume.**
- **Question it hands on:** **what is a person for, on a page, when the only thing anybody needs them for is a journey?**
- **Its cost:** nobody got stronger. A word was put to a line in the ground and taken back off the only page it was on. A party of four stood still for two hundred and five days. A man lost a season and a certainty. **And this file is one volume further from the book than it was, which is the price of deciding and it is paid here and not only in a state file.**


### Volume 08: Redroot Homes (Chapters 351–400)

- **Central pressure:** Earthside players and Veyran families build a permanent town, then discover that good intentions do not settle land, food, debt, or authority.
- **Midpoint:** The Redroot council rejects Adrian’s proposed constitution because it preserves his emergency power.
- **Climax:** A crop and water crisis forces the community to solve the problem through local labor and divided authority. Adrian gives up control of template distribution.
- **Resolution:** Redroot becomes a functioning commons, not an Adrian project. Tamsin and Adrian’s relationship becomes openly committed but politically dangerous.
- **Question:** Who is using the fear of another Sundering to recruit ordinary people into Ivenn’s plan?

### Volume 09: RECONCILED. The line this file carried for this volume was audited and not adopted, and the candidate the Volume 08 close audited was struck; what is below is what the volume is, as `outline/volume-09.md` states it, and the decisions of record are the block above.

**The five beats this file carried for this volume were never started and have been struck, in the same act and the same words as the Volume 05 line was struck and as the Volume 07 and Volume 08 lines were struck: Ivenn’s altered prompts dividing the player groups and turning local violence into a corrected version of history; Adrian using Tamsin’s knowledge as an anchor without explaining the cost and her refusing and withdrawing; Adrian disabling the Choir’s Ascension permission and losing his System access and her returning after he accepts that her refusal must remain meaningful; official false quests stopping while the Choir keeps fragments of the First Grammar; and the question of where the Grammar learned the memory of Earth games.** **What replaces them is this file’s line and it is at `outline/volume-09.md` §1 and the decisions are in this file, and no batch improvises either.**

- **Central pressure:** a boundary has to be said out loud before it is a boundary, and the only instrument four hundred miles has for saying one is a sheet with four places under a heading, and the third of the four places is a person, and the two men the line stands between are not going to be asked, and the only page in the world that says where the line runs is going away down a road that nobody in this city can be shown one person on.
- **The document:** a second sheet out of the same office nine hundred miles off, on the same quarterly round as the return, read out in a room over a market in daylight with a date on the door, in the local language, in front of about nine people, by a person who says out loud what it is for before anybody asks. **Its four places are a place, a line, a day, and a person, and the third of them is a person, and it is the first page in this flood that has ever asked anybody to put themselves on it.**
- **Midpoint, day 425, a Saturday, the hundred and tenth of that month:** a room decides out loud, and a woman who keeps a public register names the cost first, that the third place is left empty — because a page with a person on it can be used to find them, and a page with a line on it and nobody on it can be moved by whoever comes next, and she leaves it empty anyway. **There is no notice in this volume and there is not going to be an eighth, so the room is the nine who turned up and the decision is made faster than it should be, and the chapter says so in narration.**
- **Climax, day 438, a Friday, the hundred and twenty-third of that month:** a sheet with four lines on it and nothing in the third column goes out of this city on its date, and a man of about thirty-nine is right about it in about four seconds and is thanked by nobody, and the wall says one rule and the man of about thirty-four refuses it in about four seconds and what it costs him is his own ninth line. **No document is defeated. Nothing is solved. Nobody gets stronger.**
- **Resolution:** one of the four lines comes back moved, in a hand nobody in this city writes, with no column on the page for the person who moved it, and **no chapter of the volume traces it, asks about it, or says in any mouth that anybody moved it.** The register carries a second line with a day on it and nothing under the day. The third column stays empty on all fifty days.
- **Question it hands on:** *what is a line for, on a page, when the only thing anybody will use it for is to move it* — asked on day 445, in the same room, by the same woman who asked on day 346 and on day 396, to the room and not to anybody, and **not answered.** The three questions rhyme on purpose and none of them is answered.
- **What it costs, plainly.** The series loses a placement of Ivenn, which was **already** a debt under the Volume 07 and Volume 08 records and is not a new loss; it loses a system beat about a permission being disabled, which was the prompt-being-edited debt and was already unpaid; and it loses a mid-point in which the consent fracture is mended and a relationship milestone is paid, **and the consent fracture stays unmended and no relationship milestone is paid and the fifth condition is not given.** It does not lose Ivenn Marrow, the First Grammar, the Single Witness, the Quiet Choir, the return crisis, the settlement labelled unwritten, the sister, Northstar Civic, Celia Rusk, the twelve civic seals, the Charter of Two Worlds, `outline/ending.md`, the antagonist ladder or the relationship milestones, because none of those is introduced by this line. **No new final enemy is introduced and the ending does not move.** What is gained is a page that asks a person to put themselves on it, a room that decides together to leave a column empty, and a line that comes back moved with nothing on the page that says whose it was.

### Volume 10: RECONCILED. The line this file carried for this volume was audited and STRUCK; and the title this file carried for it is STRUCK WITH IT, and what is below is what the volume is, as `outline/volume-10.md` states it, and the decisions of record are the block at the foot of this file.

**THE SIX THINGS THIS ENTRY CARRIED ARE STRUCK, in the same act and the same words as the Volume 05 line was struck and as the Volume 07, 08 and 09 lines were struck.** The party travelling beneath a place to find a source of the interface; Adrian seeing that the System's safety grammar was assembled from several memories; Adrian destroying a false archive, losing a personal memory, and allowing Ivenn to escape with a fragment; Earthside governments learning the bridge is real and a joint Memory Compact beginning; the question of who will use public truth to build a bridge and who will use it to build a war; and the title, which is a plan title and not a name, and which was struck because a name in this series is a man's answer spoken once in a room in daylight with a date on the door and this file's Volume 10 entry had no such page behind it. **The place-name debt the entry carried in its own words is discharged by the strike and not by another note, and the reason is at `outline/volume-10.md` §19.4: a plan line which says out loud that it contains a thing an earlier decision struck is a plan line that has been carrying its own known fault for a volume, and a plan that carries a known fault is a plan whose other sentences cannot be trusted.** **What is kept from the strike is at `outline/volume-10.md` §19.4 item 4 and it is three sentences long: the sense that a thing made by somebody else is going to be used against the people it is about, and that the people it is about are not in the room; a figure somebody four hundred miles off made, about places in this city, with no person on it; and a false account that could keep forcing one history if preserved and could reignite a lock if destroyed.** All three are in a room with a date on the door. None of them is a journey, a loss of memory, a destruction or an escape.

- **Central pressure:** a board in a market that has stood unwritten for the whole of this flood is the only instrument in this city that has not lied to anybody, and a figure has come up the road that this city cannot check, and there is nothing in four hundred miles to put two numbers about the same thing side by side except that board, and the man who washes it every morning has decided not to wash the wall above it, and a room is going to write on it, and the chapter in which they do is the one where somebody says out loud what that board stops being the moment it has something written on it, and nobody tells him he is right.
- **The document:** a sheet out of the same office nine hundred miles off, on the same quarterly round as the return, read out in a room over a market in daylight with a date on the door, in the local language, in front of about nine people, by a person who says out loud what it is for before anybody asks. **It has a heading and ONE place under it, and the one place is for a figure, and it is the first page in this flood with one place under a heading instead of four.** The return is a district, a population, a rota and a share. The Crown's schedule is a date, a gate, a holder and the days. The sheet that went out on day 438 is a place, a line, a day and a person. **The one-place sheet is a figure and nothing else, the name of the thing the figure is about is not on the page, and the four forms are never joined.**
- **The finding:** this city keeps its own figure for the same kind of thing. **The two do not match, and neither of them is wrong, and the reason they do not match is a word: one of them is a count of places and the other is a count of people, and there is no instrument in four hundred miles that can turn one into the other except going to every place on foot. THE WORD FOR THE UNIT IS AT ZERO ACROSS ALL FOUR HUNDRED AND FIFTY CHAPTER FILES, and the words the line is built on are not, and both halves of that are published.**
- **Midpoint, day 475, a Sunday, the hundred and sixtieth of that month, the nearer of the two middle days:** a room decides out loud, and a woman who keeps a public register names the cost first, that this city's own figure goes on the store's board in chalk beside the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day, and that the two sets of figures stay side by side and neither is rubbed out. **There is no notice in this volume and there is not going to be an eighth, so the room is the nine who turned up and the decision is made faster than it should be, and the chapter says so in narration, and this is the third volume running that has paid for the spent notice at a midpoint.**
- **Climax, day 490, a Monday, the hundred and seventy-fifth of that month:** this city's figure goes onto a public wall in a market, about four people read it and none of them can say which set of figures is right, and the wall says one rule and the man who has washed that board every morning since the seventh week of this flood refuses it in about four seconds, and what it costs him is the reason he has been keeping it clean. **No document is defeated. Nothing is solved. Nobody gets stronger. The figure goes nowhere and it stays up.**
- **Resolution:** the register carries a third line with a day in it and a figure under the day, the day being the day the figure went on the board, and the two days are named in the same sentence because they are three days apart. **The third column is empty on all fifty days. The second slate is never picked up. The figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day are still on that board on the last page and were never rubbed out. The mark that came back moved is not traced, and this phase was free to trace it and declined, and the cost of declining is a hole in the book that a reader now carries through a whole volume.**
- **Question it hands on:** *what is a word for, in a count, when the only thing anybody will use it for is to make two numbers the same* — asked on day 498, in the same room, **by the woman who walked a market on two feet asking four strangers and has stopped, to one man and not to the room, and he does not answer it and the room does not hear it.** **The rhyme of person, fence and line is not continued and not improved on, and the woman who asked all three of those does not ask a fourth in this volume, and the cost of that is the series' most legible structural thread going quiet for fifty days.**
- **What it costs, plainly.** The series loses a journey, a memory to spend, a destruction, an escape, an antagonist reveal, a relationship milestone, a public-truth beat and a place-name, **all of which were scheduled here before the method was decided and none of which is owed by anybody.** **The consent fracture stays unmended, no relationship milestone is paid, the fifth condition is not given, and Ivenn Marrow is neither placed nor absent — his placement is a debt this block inherits and does not pay and does not schedule.** What is gained is a page with one place under a heading, a board that has stood unwritten since the two hundred and twenty-fifth day of this flood being made to say something, a count of places and a count of people that do not match and cannot be reconciled, and a unit. **None of it is a power. Some of it is the price of a power. All of it is heavier than a power.**

- **Resolution:** The bridge is no longer a secret to Earth, but neither world accepts the other’s version of the arrangement.
- **Question:** Can two civilizations recognize each other as political partners rather than mirrors and threats?
